It’s not just random mistakes—someone has been tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control issues, but that doesn’t fit with what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been thinking that someone is intentionally trying to harm your work?\n\nPatient: It’s been about two months now. That’s when I really became sure that people were tracking my revisions and putting errors into the data on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about people intentionally putting errors into your datasets changed at all over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Not really. If anything, I’ve become more sure of it. I keep seeing the same kind of errors show up after my revisions, and it feels like they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about other areas of your life, outside of this contract?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. It’s mainly this contract—the environmental modeling work. That’s where I’m seeing the revisions being tracked and the dataset errors showing up, like someone’s trying to make it look like I can’t lead it. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period, even briefly, where you doubted that someone was intentionally putting errors into your datasets?\n\nPatient: Maybe once or twice for a minute, when my wife or one of the junior analysts says it’s just version-control issues. I’ll think, okay, maybe that could explain one file. But then I see the same kind of errors after my revisions, and I go right back to thinking someone is tracking what I’m doing and planting them to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about someone intentionally sabotaging your work ever changed significantly, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: Not significantly, no. I mean, I’ll hesitate for a moment when my wife or the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but then I look at the way the revisions get tracked and the errors show up again, and I’m pretty sure they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible, but I really don’t think so. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets right after, it feels too specific. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control mistakes, but that doesn’t explain it to me. I’m pretty sure someone on that contract is doing it to make me look like I can’t lead the work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s an environmental modeling project, and I’m pretty sure people involved with it have been tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work.\n\nMy wife and some of the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control mistakes, but it doesn’t feel like that to me. It feels deliberate.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is intentionally trying to harm you or your work?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think it’s intentional. It’s not just random mistakes—someone has been tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control issues, but that doesn’t fit with what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been thinking that someone is intentionally trying to harm your work?\n\nPatient: It’s been about two months now. That’s when I really became sure that people were tracking my revisions and putting errors into the data on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about people intentionally putting errors into your datasets changed at all over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Not really. If anything, I’ve become more sure of it. I keep seeing the same kind of errors show up after my revisions, and it feels like they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about other areas of your life, outside of this contract?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. It’s mainly this contract—the environmental modeling work. That’s where I’m seeing the revisions being tracked and the dataset errors showing up, like someone’s trying to make it look like I can’t lead it. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period, even briefly, where you doubted that someone was intentionally putting errors into your datasets?\n\nPatient: Maybe once or twice for a minute, when my wife or one of the junior analysts says it’s just version-control issues. I’ll think, okay, maybe that could explain one file. But then I see the same kind of errors after my revisions, and I go right back to thinking someone is tracking what I’m doing and planting them to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about someone intentionally sabotaging your work ever changed significantly, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: Not significantly, no. I mean, I’ll hesitate for a moment when my wife or the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but then I look at the way the revisions get tracked and the errors show up again, and I’m pretty sure they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible, but I really don’t think so. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets right after, it feels too specific. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control mistakes, but that doesn’t explain it to me. I’m pretty sure someone on that contract is doing it to make me look like I can’t lead the work.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I’d say I’m pretty certain. Not 100 percent in some abstract sense, but… close. It just keeps lining up too neatly — my revisions get tracked, then errors show up in the datasets, and it makes me look like I can’t manage the contract. My wife and some of the junior analysts say it’s probably version-control problems, but that doesn’t really fit what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any other thoughts that might seem unusual or hard to believe to others, outside of this contract issue?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. The main thing is the contract — the revisions being tracked and the errors showing up in the datasets. That’s the part people seem to have trouble believing when I say it’s being done on purpose to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your assessment that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: Some people wouldn’t, no. My wife doesn’t agree — she keeps saying it’s probably version-control mistakes. And a couple of the junior analysts say the same thing. But I still think they’re missing what I’m seeing. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets… it feels intentional to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m having a serious problem with a contract I’ve been working on. It’s an environmental modeling project, and I’m pretty sure people involved with it have been tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work.\n\nMy wife and some of the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control mistakes, but it doesn’t feel like that to me. It feels deliberate.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is intentionally trying to harm you or your work?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think it’s intentional. It’s not just random mistakes—someone has been tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control issues, but that doesn’t fit with what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been thinking that someone is intentionally trying to harm your work?\n\nPatient: It’s been about two months now. That’s when I really became sure that people were tracking my revisions and putting errors into the data on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about people intentionally putting errors into your datasets changed at all over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Not really. If anything, I’ve become more sure of it. I keep seeing the same kind of errors show up after my revisions, and it feels like they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about other areas of your life, outside of this contract?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. It’s mainly this contract—the environmental modeling work. That’s where I’m seeing the revisions being tracked and the dataset errors showing up, like someone’s trying to make it look like I can’t lead it. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period, even briefly, where you doubted that someone was intentionally putting errors into your datasets?\n\nPatient: Maybe once or twice for a minute, when my wife or one of the junior analysts says it’s just version-control issues. I’ll think, okay, maybe that could explain one file. But then I see the same kind of errors after my revisions, and I go right back to thinking someone is tracking what I’m doing and planting them to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about someone intentionally sabotaging your work ever changed significantly, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: Not significantly, no. I mean, I’ll hesitate for a moment when my wife or the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but then I look at the way the revisions get tracked and the errors show up again, and I’m pretty sure they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control mistakes, but that doesn’t explain it to me. I’m pretty sure someone on that contract is doing it to make me look like I can’t lead the work.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I’d say I’m pretty certain. Not 100 percent in some abstract sense, but… close. It just keeps lining up too neatly — my revisions get tracked, then errors show up in the datasets, and it makes me look like I can’t manage the contract. The main thing is the contract — the revisions being tracked and the errors showing up in the datasets. That’s the part people seem to have trouble believing when I say it’s being done on purpose to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your assessment that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: Some people wouldn’t, no. That’s when I really became sure that people were tracking my revisions and putting errors into the data on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about people intentionally putting errors into your datasets changed at all over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Not really. If anything, I’ve become more sure of it. I keep seeing the same kind of errors show up after my revisions, and it feels like they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about other areas of your life, outside of this contract?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. It’s mainly this contract—the environmental modeling work. That’s where I’m seeing the revisions being tracked and the dataset errors showing up, like someone’s trying to make it look like I can’t lead it. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period, even briefly, where you doubted that someone was intentionally putting errors into your datasets?\n\nPatient: Maybe once or twice for a minute, when my wife or one of the junior analysts says it’s just version-control issues. I’ll think, okay, maybe that could explain one file. But then I see the same kind of errors after my revisions, and I go right back to thinking someone is tracking what I’m doing and planting them to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about someone intentionally sabotaging your work ever changed significantly, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: Not significantly, no. I mean, I’ll hesitate for a moment when my wife or the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but then I look at the way the revisions get tracked and the errors show up again, and I’m pretty sure they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible, but I really don’t think so. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets right after, it feels too specific. The main thing is the contract — the revisions being tracked and the errors showing up in the datasets. That’s the part people seem to have trouble believing when I say it’s being done on purpose to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your assessment that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: Some people wouldn’t, no. My wife doesn’t agree — she keeps saying it’s probably version-control mistakes. And a couple of the junior analysts say the same thing. But I still think they’re missing what I’m seeing. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets… it feels intentional to me.\n\nDoctor: Is your belief that someone is sabotaging your work part of a larger plan or scheme that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a larger scheme. What I’ve figured out is more specific: on that environmental modeling contract, it seems like people are tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but that explanation doesn’t really fit to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work at the contract are also out to harm you in other ways?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they’re trying to harm me in some other way, like physically or anything. What I’m focused on is the contract. I’m pretty sure someone there is tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and some of the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people involved in sabotaging your work are connected to any other events or situations in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d connect it to other parts of my life. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about other areas of your life, outside of this contract?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. It’s mainly this contract—the environmental modeling work. That’s where I’m seeing the revisions being tracked and the dataset errors showing up, like someone’s trying to make it look like I can’t lead it. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period, even briefly, where you doubted that someone was intentionally putting errors into your datasets?\n\nPatient: Maybe once or twice for a minute, when my wife or one of the junior analysts says it’s just version-control issues. I’ll think, okay, maybe that could explain one file. But then I see the same kind of errors after my revisions, and I go right back to thinking someone is tracking what I’m doing and planting them to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about someone intentionally sabotaging your work ever changed significantly, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: Not significantly, no. I mean, I’ll hesitate for a moment when my wife or the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but then I look at the way the revisions get tracked and the errors show up again, and I’m pretty sure they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible, but I really don’t think so. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets right after, it feels too specific. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control mistakes, but that doesn’t explain it to me. I’m pretty sure someone on that contract is doing it to make me look like I can’t lead the work.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I’d say I’m pretty certain. Not 100 percent in some abstract sense, but… close. It just keeps lining up too neatly — my revisions get tracked, then errors show up in the datasets, and it makes me look like I can’t manage the contract. My wife and some of the junior analysts say it’s probably version-control problems, but that doesn’t really fit what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any other thoughts that might seem unusual or hard to believe to others, outside of this contract issue?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. The main thing is the contract — the revisions being tracked and the errors showing up in the datasets. That’s the part people seem to have trouble believing when I say it’s being done on purpose to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your assessment that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: Some people wouldn’t, no. My wife doesn’t agree — she keeps saying it’s probably version-control mistakes. And a couple of the junior analysts say the same thing. But I still think they’re missing what I’m seeing. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets… it feels intentional to me.\n\nDoctor: Is your belief that someone is sabotaging your work part of a larger plan or scheme that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a larger scheme. What I’ve figured out is more specific: on that environmental modeling contract, it seems like people are tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but that explanation doesn’t really fit to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work at the contract are also out to harm you in other ways?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they’re trying to harm me in some other way, like physically or anything. What I’m focused on is the contract. I’m pretty sure someone there is tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and some of the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people involved in sabotaging your work are connected to any other events or situations in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d connect it to other parts of my life. It’s really about that environmental modeling contract. I’m pretty sure people there have been tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead it. My wife and some of the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control mistakes, but that doesn’t sit right with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work are organized in any way or is it just one or a few individuals acting independently?\n\nPatient: I can’t say for sure how organized it is. It feels like it’s probably one or a few people on that contract who have access to my revisions and the datasets. They’re tracking what I change and then planting errors so it looks like I can’t manage the work. Other people keep saying it’s version-control mistakes, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work at the contract are part of a larger group that might be affecting other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s some larger group affecting the rest of my life. What I keep coming back to is that contract. I’m pretty sure people there are tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and the junior analysts say it’s version-control issues, but that doesn’t explain it to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets… it feels intentional to me.\n\nDoctor: Is your belief that someone is sabotaging your work part of a larger plan or scheme that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a larger scheme. What I’ve figured out is more specific: on that environmental modeling contract, it seems like people are tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but that explanation doesn’t really fit to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work at the contract are also out to harm you in other ways?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they’re trying to harm me in some other way, like physically or anything. What I’m focused on is the contract. I’m pretty sure someone there is tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and some of the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people involved in sabotaging your work are connected to any other events or situations in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d connect it to other parts of my life. It’s really about that environmental modeling contract. I’m pretty sure people there have been tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead it. My wife and some of the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control mistakes, but that doesn’t sit right with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work are organized in any way or is it just one or a few individuals acting independently?\n\nPatient: I can’t say for sure how organized it is. It feels like it’s probably one or a few people on that contract who have access to my revisions and the datasets. They’re tracking what I change and then planting errors so it looks like I can’t manage the work. Other people keep saying it’s version-control mistakes, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work at the contract are part of a larger group that might be affecting other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s some larger group affecting the rest of my life. What I keep coming back to is that contract. I’m pretty sure people there are tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and the junior analysts say it’s version-control issues, but that doesn’t explain it to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few days, where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m having a serious problem with a contract I’ve been working on. It’s an environmental modeling project, and I’m pretty sure people involved with it have been tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work.\n\nMy wife and some of the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control mistakes, but it doesn’t feel like that to me. It feels deliberate.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is intentionally trying to harm you or your work?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think it’s intentional. It’s not just random mistakes—someone has been tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control issues, but that doesn’t fit with what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been thinking that someone is intentionally trying to harm your work?\n\nPatient: It’s been about two months now. That’s when I really became sure that people were tracking my revisions and putting errors into the data on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about people intentionally putting errors into your datasets changed at all over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Not really. If anything, I’ve become more sure of it. I keep seeing the same kind of errors show up after my revisions, and it feels like they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about other areas of your life, outside of this contract?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. It’s mainly this contract—the environmental modeling work. That’s where I’m seeing the revisions being tracked and the dataset errors showing up, like someone’s trying to make it look like I can’t lead it. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period, even briefly, where you doubted that someone was intentionally putting errors into your datasets?\n\nPatient: Maybe once or twice for a minute, when my wife or one of the junior analysts says it’s just version-control issues. I’ll think, okay, maybe that could explain one file. But then I see the same kind of errors after my revisions, and I go right back to thinking someone is tracking what I’m doing and planting them to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about someone intentionally sabotaging your work ever changed significantly, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: Not significantly, no. I mean, I’ll hesitate for a moment when my wife or the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but then I look at the way the revisions get tracked and the errors show up again, and I’m pretty sure they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible, but I really don’t think so. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets right after, it feels too specific. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control mistakes, but that doesn’t explain it to me. I’m pretty sure someone on that contract is doing it to make me look like I can’t lead the work.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I’d say I’m pretty certain. Not 100 percent in some abstract sense, but… close. It just keeps lining up too neatly — my revisions get tracked, then errors show up in the datasets, and it makes me look like I can’t manage the contract. My wife and some of the junior analysts say it’s probably version-control problems, but that doesn’t really fit what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any other thoughts that might seem unusual or hard to believe to others, outside of this contract issue?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. The main thing is the contract — the revisions being tracked and the errors showing up in the datasets. That’s the part people seem to have trouble believing when I say it’s being done on purpose to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your assessment that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: Some people wouldn’t, no. My wife doesn’t agree — she keeps saying it’s probably version-control mistakes. And a couple of the junior analysts say the same thing. But I still think they’re missing what I’m seeing. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets… it feels intentional to me.\n\nDoctor: Is your belief that someone is sabotaging your work part of a larger plan or scheme that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a larger scheme. What I’ve figured out is more specific: on that environmental modeling contract, it seems like people are tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but that explanation doesn’t really fit to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work at the contract are also out to harm you in other ways?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they’re trying to harm me in some other way, like physically or anything. What I’m focused on is the contract. I’m pretty sure someone there is tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and some of the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people involved in sabotaging your work are connected to any other events or situations in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d connect it to other parts of my life. It’s really about that environmental modeling contract. I’m pretty sure people there have been tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead it. My wife and some of the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control mistakes, but that doesn’t sit right with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work are organized in any way or is it just one or a few individuals acting independently?\n\nPatient: I can’t say for sure how organized it is. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’m having a serious problem with a contract I’ve been working on. It’s an environmental modeling project, and I’m pretty sure people involved with it have been tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work.\n\nMy wife and some of the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control mistakes, but it doesn’t feel like that to me. It feels deliberate.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is intentionally trying to harm you or your work?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think it’s intentional. It’s not just random mistakes—someone has been tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control issues, but that doesn’t fit with what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been thinking that someone is intentionally trying to harm your work?\n\nPatient: It’s been about two months now. That’s when I really became sure that people were tracking my revisions and putting errors into the data on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about people intentionally putting errors into your datasets changed at all over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Not really. If anything, I’ve become more sure of it. I keep seeing the same kind of errors show up after my revisions, and it feels like they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about other areas of your life, outside of this contract?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. It’s mainly this contract—the environmental modeling work. That’s where I’m seeing the revisions being tracked and the dataset errors showing up, like someone’s trying to make it look like I can’t lead it. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period, even briefly, where you doubted that someone was intentionally putting errors into your datasets?\n\nPatient: Maybe once or twice for a minute, when my wife or one of the junior analysts says it’s just version-control issues. I’ll think, okay, maybe that could explain one file. But then I see the same kind of errors after my revisions, and I go right back to thinking someone is tracking what I’m doing and planting them to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about someone intentionally sabotaging your work ever changed significantly, even if only for a short time?\n\nPatient: Not significantly, no. I mean, I’ll hesitate for a moment when my wife or the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but then I look at the way the revisions get tracked and the errors show up again, and I’m pretty sure they’re being planted to make me look like I can’t lead the contract.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be completely wrong about someone intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely wrong? I guess anything is possible, but I really don’t think so. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets right after, it feels too specific. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s version-control mistakes, but that doesn’t explain it to me. I’m pretty sure someone on that contract is doing it to make me look like I can’t lead the work.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I’d say I’m pretty certain. Not 100 percent in some abstract sense, but… close. It just keeps lining up too neatly — my revisions get tracked, then errors show up in the datasets, and it makes me look like I can’t manage the contract. My wife and some of the junior analysts say it’s probably version-control problems, but that doesn’t really fit what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any other thoughts that might seem unusual or hard to believe to others, outside of this contract issue?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. The main thing is the contract — the revisions being tracked and the errors showing up in the datasets. That’s the part people seem to have trouble believing when I say it’s being done on purpose to make me look bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your assessment that someone is intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: Some people wouldn’t, no. My wife doesn’t agree — she keeps saying it’s probably version-control mistakes. And a couple of the junior analysts say the same thing. But I still think they’re missing what I’m seeing. The way the revisions get tracked and then errors show up in the datasets… it feels intentional to me.\n\nDoctor: Is your belief that someone is sabotaging your work part of a larger plan or scheme that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a larger scheme. What I’ve figured out is more specific: on that environmental modeling contract, it seems like people are tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes, but that explanation doesn’t really fit to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work at the contract are also out to harm you in other ways?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that they’re trying to harm me in some other way, like physically or anything. What I’m focused on is the contract. I’m pretty sure someone there is tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and some of the junior analysts say it’s just version-control mistakes, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people involved in sabotaging your work are connected to any other events or situations in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d connect it to other parts of my life. It’s really about that environmental modeling contract. I’m pretty sure people there have been tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead it. My wife and some of the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control mistakes, but that doesn’t sit right with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work are organized in any way or is it just one or a few individuals acting independently?\n\nPatient: I can’t say for sure how organized it is. It feels like it’s probably one or a few people on that contract who have access to my revisions and the datasets. They’re tracking what I change and then planting errors so it looks like I can’t manage the work. Other people keep saying it’s version-control mistakes, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work at the contract are part of a larger group that might be affecting other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s some larger group affecting the rest of my life. What I keep coming back to is that contract. I’m pretty sure people there are tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and the junior analysts say it’s version-control issues, but that doesn’t explain it to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few days, where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, there might be hours where I’m focused on something else and not thinking about it, but when I look back at the contract work, it’s still there. The revisions get tracked, then errors show up in the datasets, and it looks like I can’t lead it. People tell me it’s just version-control problems, but I don’t think that explains it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your belief about someone sabotaging your work could be a mistaken perception?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because my wife and the junior analysts keep saying it could just be version-control mistakes. But honestly, that doesn’t fit what I’m seeing. My revisions get tracked, and then errors show up in the datasets in a way that makes me look like I can’t lead the work. So no, I don’t really think I’m mistaken about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you could be persuaded that someone is not intentionally sabotaging your work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. The revisions are tracked, then errors show up in the datasets, and it ends up making me look like I can’t lead the contract. Other people keep saying it’s version-control mistakes, but I don’t buy that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people sabotaging your work at the contract are part of a larger group that might be affecting other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s some larger group affecting the rest of my life. What I keep coming back to is that contract. I’m pretty sure people there are tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work. My wife and the junior analysts say it’s version-control issues, but that doesn’t explain it to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few days, where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: Not really, no.
